Otherwise save the canonical plan at `docs/superpowers/plans/YYYY-MM-DD-<kebab-case-feature-name>.md` using the current date and a non-colliding filename.\n- After saving, link the file in the final response and provide only a concise handoff summary unless the user asks to repeat the full plan in chat.\n- Treat an omitted destination as covered by this convention. Process skills first\n2. Implementation skills second\n\nExamples:\n\n- \"Make this button blue\" -> inspect the relevant file, make the small edit, do targeted verification\n- \"Update this prompt sentence\" -> edit the prompt directly and verify the final text\n- \"Build a feature\" -> `brainstorming` with a PM pass first, then `writing-plans`, then execution; consider approval-gated `agentic-user-journey-testing` for app workflows\n- \"Fix this bug\" -> `systematic-debugging`, then the relevant implementation skill; if the root cause is meaningful or systemic, finish with `learning-from-bugs`\n- \"Review this change\" -> `requesting-code-review`\n\n## Examples Of User Override\n\n- If a task feels risky and the user did not ask for extra repository isolation, keep working in the current repository unless the user explicitly instructs otherwise.\n- If a stack supports file-based routing but the user says not to use it, choose an explicit routing approach instead.\n- If a framework strongly defaults to file-based routing, such as Next.js App Router, use the framework-native routing model unless there is a good reason not to.\n- If a frontend skill suggests `shadcn/ui` but the user wants another design system, follow the user.\n\n## Red Flags\n\n- \"I need a whole workflow for this tiny edit\" -> no. Use the smallest responsible process.\n- \"This is simple, so I need the whole process anyway\" -> no. Tiny mechanical edits should stay tiny.\n- \"I'll create extra repository isolation just to be safe\" -> forbidden unless the user clearly asked for it.\n- \"I already know the skill\" -> read the current version before relying on it\n---\nname: client-server-state-invariants\ndescription: Use when planning, building, reviewing, or debugging React apps with server state, optimistic UI, caches, polling, SSE/WebSocket streams, background jobs, persistence, or client/server synchronization\n---\n\n# Client Server State Invariants\n\nReact bugs often expose deeper ownership mistakes. When client state, server state, cache state, stream state, and persisted state all exist, every plan needs explicit invariants.\n\n## Core Rule\n\nNo UI surface may treat data as authoritative unless the payload proves ownership, freshness, completeness, ordering, and terminal state.\n\nIf those words are missing from the plan, the plan is probably baking in a race condition.\n\n## Planning Checklist\n\nFor every React app that reads server/runtime state, the plan must answer:\n\n1. **Owner:** What owns this state: server, client manager, URL, cache, stream, local storage, or worker/runtime?\n2. **Token:** What owner token protects async results: `requestId`, `runId`, `projectPath`, `operationId`, `workerId + seq`, or equivalent?\n3. **Provenance:** Is data server-authoritative, optimistic, cached preview, partial update, or historical fallback?\n4. **Completeness:** Does the payload declare what scope it is complete for? What may it erase?\n5. **Ordering:** What stable order is used: monotonic seq, `(createdAt, id)`, version, or cursor?\n6. **State machine:** What are the legal states, terminal states, and transitions?\n7. **Events:** Which server decisions are named events rather than client-side derivations?\n8. **Loading shape:** Which states are bootstrap, first load, incremental refresh, reconnect, resync, recovery, and terminal error?\n9. **Fallbacks:** Which legacy/fallback rows can render, and only after what authority is loaded?\n10. **Tests:** Which race, stale-response, event-sequence, reconnect, and merge tests prove the invariants?\n\n## Invariants To Require\n\n- Cached and optimistic data may pre-render, but cannot satisfy full-load gates.\n- Partial snapshots may add or refine state, but may not erase optimistic objects, delivered user input, read markers, or stream entries unless they declare complete scope.\n- Every async response checks that its owner token still matches before mutating visible state.\n- Mutation success handlers may navigate or clear UI only if they still own the current context, unless the action explicitly creates a new selected context.\n- Timers and polling callbacks re-check ownership immediately before acting.\n- Server decisions are emitted as named events; the client does not infer decisions by diffing snapshots.\n- SSE/WebSocket streams have event ids, replay/resume, and an explicit resync path.\n- Loading booleans are split by lifecycle stage. A leaf stream refresh must not unmount the whole page.\n- Append-only content has one writer, one reader, stable ids, and monotonic cursors.\n- Fallback DB rows or historical rows are gated behind stream loading and deduped by stable id plus safe equivalence.\n- Status classifiers distinguish active, runnable, blocked, awaiting user, terminal, stale, and recoverable states.\n- \"Latest\" queries have deterministic tie-breakers. Timestamp-only ordering is not enough.\n- Hot paths use bounded reads and do not synchronously load large bodies, bridge probes, schema setup, or heavy app graphs.\n- Process-local caches have explicit TTL/size bounds and invalidation rules.\n\n## Test Matrix\n\nPick the rows that match the work. Do not rely on one happy-path UI test.\n\n| Risk | Test shape |\n| --- | --- |\n| Stale async response | Start request A, switch owner, resolve A, assert it cannot mutate B |\n| Optimistic object erased | Create local optimistic state, apply stale/partial snapshot, assert object remains |\n| Cache masquerades as loaded | Hydrate cache preview, assert full-load gate waits for server authority |\n| Mutation steals navigation | Start mutation, switch selection, resolve mutation, assert focus stays put |\n| Timer/poll stale-fire | Use fake timers or deferred promises; callback must re-check owner before acting |\n| Snapshot merge erases stream | Apply partial snapshot missing known entries, assert entries/read markers remain |\n| Event stream gap | Bootstrap snapshot, emit events during disconnect, reconnect with last id, assert replay or resync |\n| Server decision invisible | Exercise branch and assert named event such as `blocked`, `refused`, `gave_up`, or `error.surfaced` |\n| State classifier wrong | Table-test every status into active/runnable/terminal/recoverable buckets |\n| Fallback duplicates stream | Load fallback rows then stream entries, assert no duplicate or false chronology |\n| Hot path bloats | Assert snapshots carry cursors not bodies; route returns before DB/heavy imports when possible |\n| Timestamp tie | Insert equal timestamps, assert deterministic order by id/seq |\n\nUse headless HTTP/SSE tests for control-plane decisions. Use React/unit tests for manager merging and ownership guards. Use Playwright only for real UI layout, focus, scroll, mobile computed styles, and visible affordances.\n\n## Refuse These Shapes\n\n- one `isLoading` boolean for bootstrap, refresh, reconnect, and recovery;\n- cache or optimistic data treated as authoritative without provenance;\n- async callbacks that mutate state without checking owner tokens;\n- client-side derivation of server decisions;\n- snapshot diffs used as the only way to know what happened;\n- event streams without ids or resume;\n- timestamp-only \"latest\" queries;\n- parallel transcript or persistence surfaces that the frontend must reconcile;\n- unbounded process caches introduced to hide slow storage;\n- tests that prove only the DOM result when the bug is a server decision.\n\n## Review Prompt\n\nAsk this before implementation starts:\n\n> What can arrive late, out of order, partially, twice, or after the user changes context, and what test proves it cannot corrupt visible state?\n"

    "text": "---\nname: requesting-code-review\ndescription: Use when completing tasks, implementing major features, or before merging to verify work meets requirements\n---\n\n# Requesting Code Review\n\nDispatch ultrapowers:code-reviewer subagent to catch issues before they cascade. The reviewer gets precisely crafted context for evaluation — never your session's history. This keeps the reviewer focused on the work product, not your thought process, and preserves your own context for continued work.\n\n**Core principle:** Review early, review often.\n\n## When to Request Review\n\n**Mandatory:**\n- After each task in subagent-driven development\n- After completing major feature\n- Before merge to main\n\n**Optional but valuable:**\n- When stuck (fresh perspective)\n- Before refactoring (baseline check)\n- After fixing complex bug\n\n## How to Request\n\n**1. Get git SHAs:**\n```bash\nBASE_SHA=$(git rev-parse HEAD~1)  # or origin/main\nHEAD_SHA=$(git rev-parse HEAD)\n```\n\n**2. Dispatch code-reviewer subagent:**\n\nUse Task tool with ultrapowers:code-reviewer type, fill template at `code-reviewer.md`\n\n**Placeholders:**\n- `{WHAT_WAS_IMPLEMENTED}` - What you just built\n- `{PLAN_OR_REQUIREMENTS}` - What it should do\n- `{BASE_SHA}` - Starting commit\n- `{HEAD_SHA}` - Ending commit\n- `{DESCRIPTION}` - Brief summary\n\n**3. Act on feedback:**\n- Fix Critical issues immediately\n- Fix Important issues before proceeding\n- Note Minor issues for later\n- Push back if reviewer is wrong (with reasoning)\n\n## Example\n\n```\n[Just completed Task 2: Add verification function]\n\nYou: Let me request code review before proceeding.\n\nBASE_SHA=$(git log --oneline | grep \"Task 1\" | head -1 | awk '{print $1}')\nHEAD_SHA=$(git rev-parse HEAD)\n\n[Dispatch ultrapowers:code-reviewer subagent]\n  WHAT_WAS_IMPLEMENTED: Verification and repair functions for conversation index\n  PLAN_OR_REQUIREMENTS: Task 2 from docs/superpowers/plans/deployment-plan.md\n  BASE_SHA: a7981ec\n  HEAD_SHA: 3df7661\n  DESCRIPTION: Added verifyIndex() and repairIndex() with 4 issue types\n\n[Subagent returns]:\n  Strengths: Clean architecture, real tests\n  Issues:\n    Important: Missing progress indicators\n    Minor: Magic number (100) for reporting interval\n  Assessment: Ready to proceed\n\nYou: [Fix progress indicators]\n[Continue to Task 3]\n```\n\n## Integration with Workflows\n\n**Subagent-Driven Development:**\n- Review after EACH task\n- Catch issues before they compound\n- Fix before moving to next task\n\n**Executing Plans:**\n- Review after each batch (3 tasks)\n- Get feedback, apply, continue\n\n**Ad-Hoc Development:**\n- Review before merge\n- Review when stuck\n\n## Red Flags\n\n**Never:**\n- Skip review because \"it's simple\"\n- Ignore Critical issues\n- Proceed with unfixed Important issues\n- Argue with valid technical feedback\n\n**If reviewer wrong:**\n- Push back with technical reasoning\n- Show code/tests that prove it works\n- Request clarification\n\nSee template at: requesting-code-review/code-reviewer.md\n---\nname: instrumenting-control-planes\ndescription: Use when building, planning, reviewing, or debugging apps with backend decisions, agent workflows, long-running jobs, SSE/WebSocket streams, recovery paths, deletes, retries, or user-visible failures\n---\n\n# Instrumenting Control Planes\n\nBuild the control plane as a product surface. If the server decides, refuses, retries, spawns, deletes, recovers, gives up, or fails, that decision must be observable by users, operators, and tests.\n\nIf a React UI consumes the control plane through server state, optimistic UI, caches, polling, SSE/WebSocket streams, or persisted runtime state, also use `client-server-state-invariants`.\n\n## Core Rule\n\nEvery meaningful server-side state transition emits a named, durable event. The UI and tests consume the same production event surface.\n\nDo not rely on snapshot diffs, rendered DOM state, logs only, or client-side re-derivation to prove what the server decided.\n\n## Hot Path Rule\n\nNo user bootstrap, catalog, status, reconnect, or recovery request may depend on a lock-prone database write path or external health probe finishing. Serve these paths from a bounded read model, memory cache, append-only event log, or stale-but-marked snapshot; queue reconciliation writes outside the request. Every dependency on a database or bridge call in a hot path needs a short timeout, cancellation, and an explicit degraded response.\n\nIf SQLite, local files, or a single-writer store is used, assume writer contention can freeze readers at the worst moment. Use one writer, WAL/busy-timeout discipline, short transactions, bounded queues, and headless chaos tests that run bootstrap/reload while writes are active. A 25s UI request is a control-plane failure, even if the database eventually returns.\n\n## Event Contract\n\nFor each backend or agent workflow, define:\n\n- named events for decisions: `worker.spawned`, `worker.reattached`, `plan.ready`, `plan.review.blocked`, `recovery.gave_up`, `conversation.delete_failed`;\n- payloads with stable ids, `prev`/`next` status when relevant, reason codes, and enough context to debug;\n- `error.surfaced` or an equivalent user-facing failure event for every failure the user should know about;\n- durable persistence or a bounded replay buffer so short disconnects do not erase history;\n- a scriptable read path: CLI, HTTP endpoint, sqlite query, or event log command.\n\nIf a decision cannot be asserted from this surface, the design is incomplete.\n\nIf debugging a control-plane failure reveals a reusable lesson, use `ultrapowers:learning-from-bugs` after the fix is verified.\n\n## Streaming Requirements\n\nFor SSE or WebSocket-like streams:\n\n- emit named events alongside any snapshot/update frame;\n- include monotonic event ids;\n- support resume from the last seen id;\n- replay missed events from a bounded buffer;\n- emit an explicit resync-required event when replay is impossible;\n- bootstrap from a snapshot, then tail events.\n\nSnapshots show current truth. Named events show why it became true.\n\n## Testing Standard\n\nControl-plane tests should usually be headless HTTP/SSE scenario tests, not browser tests. Browser automation is for UI behavior; control-plane tests assert server decisions directly.\n\nWrite scenario assertions as event transcripts:\n\n```ts\nexpect(events).toHaveEmitted(\"worker.spawned\", { runId });\nexpect(events).toHaveEmitted(\"worker.status\", { next: \"running\" });\nchaos.killServer();\nchaos.restartServer();\nexpect(events).toHaveEmitted(\"worker.reattached\", { runId });\nexpect(events).not.toHaveEmitted(\"worker.recreated\", { runId });\n```\n\nChaos belongs outside production code paths: drop connections in the client, fail fetches in the harness, restart the server process, and use seeds for reproducibility.\n\n## Refuse These Shapes\n\n- silent early returns in server methods;\n- swallowed `catch` blocks;\n- client-side derivation of server decisions;\n- response-code-only user failures;\n- event streams without ids or resume;\n- tests that infer control-plane behavior from rendered state;\n- delete handlers that do not enumerate dependent rows or emit typed failure events;\n- recovery loops without terminal events such as `gave_up` or `abandoned`;\n- request handlers that wait on inline reconciliation writes, SQLite contention, or bridge `/doctor` checks before returning bootstrap data;\n- test-only behavior branches in production code.\n\n## Design Checklist\n\nBefore implementing a backend or agent workflow, answer:\n\n1. What decisions can this workflow make?\n2. What named event is emitted for each decision, including refusal and failure?\n3. Where are events persisted or buffered?\n4. How does a client resume after disconnect?\n5. Which request paths must keep working while persistence or bridge dependencies are slow, locked, or stale?\n6. The selected snapshot was\n+926 KB, uncompressed, and live SSE updates repeated the complete 407-run / 549-\n+worker catalog even though only one conversation's live state was changing.\n+At the measured rate this was about 2.1 GB per hour for one client.\n+\n+### Root causes\n+\n+1. Selected-run SSE frames reused the complete bootstrap payload. The client\n+   already had the full catalog, but every live update resent all runs,\n+   workers, sessions, plans, and read markers.\n+2. The portable Node HTTP server did not negotiate compression for JSON or\n+   SSE, so highly repetitive state crossed the network at its raw JSON size.\n+3. Healthy clients still validated snapshots every five seconds, and selected\n+   worker/transcript streams polled every two to five seconds despite having\n+   resumable SSE wake-ups and content cursors.\n+\n+### Fix and evidence\n+\n+- Snapshot bootstrap remains complete and authoritative.\n+- Selected-run SSE frames declare `snapshotScope.catalog.complete=false` and\n+  carry only the selected run, its workers, session, plan, and read marker.\n+  The existing manager merges that slice into the bootstrapped catalog.\n+- The full payload checksum stays on the partial frame, so snapshot validation\n+  can receive `notModified` without redownloading the catalog.\n+- The runtime HTTP server gzip-compresses compressible responses, including\n+  SSE with `Z_SYNC_FLUSH` so each live frame remains immediately readable.\n+- Healthy snapshot validation moved from 5 seconds to 60 seconds. [

    "type": "input_text",
    "text": "   675\t  times row count.\n   676\t- \"It's only a sessionStorage write.\" — synchronous storage on a click handler\n   677\t  runs before the repaint that acknowledges the click.\n   678\t- \"Nothing reads this field, but it's harmless to include.\" — 142 KB per frame\n   679\t  per client of harmless.\n   680\t\n   681\t## August 27, 2026 — Mobile Network Transfer Incident\n   682\t\n   683\tThe browser-to-runner connection transferred 7,057,338 bytes in a 12-second\n   684\tsample while one selected conversation was open. The selected snapshot was\n   685\t926 KB, uncompressed, and live SSE updates repeated the complete 407-run / 549-\n   686\tworker catalog even though only one conversation's live state was changing.\n   687\tAt the measured rate this was about 2.1 GB per hour for one client.\n   688\t\n   689\t### Root causes\n   690\t\n   691\t1. Selected-run SSE frames reused the complete bootstrap payload. The client\n   692\t   already had the full catalog, but every live update resent all runs,\n   693\t   workers, sessions, plans, and read markers.\n   694\t2. The portable Node HTTP server did not negotiate compression for JSON or\n   695\t   SSE, so highly repetitive state crossed the network at its raw JSON size.\n   696\t3. Healthy clients still validated snapshots every five seconds, and selected\n   697\t   worker/transcript streams polled every two to five seconds despite having\n   698\t   resumable SSE wake-ups and content cursors.\n   699\t\n   700\t### Fix and evidence\n   701\t\n   702\t- Snapshot bootstrap remains complete and authoritative.\n   703\t- Selected-run SSE frames declare `snapshotScope.catalog.complete=false` and\n   704\t  carry only the selected run, its workers, session, plan, and read marker.\n   705\t  The existing manager merges that slice into the bootstrapped catalog.\n   706\t- The full payload checksum stays on the partial frame, so snapshot validation\n   707\t  can receive `notModified` without redownloading the catalog.\n   708\t- The runtime HTTP server gzip-compresses compressible responses, including\n   709\t  SSE with `Z_SYNC_FLUSH` so each live frame remains immediately readable.\n   710\t- Healthy snapshot validation moved from 5 seconds to 60 seconds. Worker\n   711\t  safety polling is clamped to 30 seconds while active and 60 seconds while\n   712\t  idle; SSE wake-ups still fetch new transcript content immediately.\n   713\t\n   714\tAfter restarting the real runner, the same 12-second selected-conversation\n   715\tsample transferred 4,647 wire bytes. The live update was 18.9 KB before gzip,\n   716\tand the 937 KB bootstrap used 160 KB on the wire. That is a 99.93% reduction\n   717\tin the measured live window.\n   718\t\n   719\t### Permanent rules added\n   720\t\n   721\t#### Rule 15: Bootstrap Completeness Does Not Belong In Every Live Frame\n   722\t\n   723\tBootstrap establishes the complete catalog. A run-scoped live channel should\n   724\tsend a scoped partial catalog with an explicit completeness contract and merge\n   725\tit by stable ids. Never reuse a complete bootstrap object as a convenient live\n   726\tevent payload.\n   727\t\n   728\t#### Rule 16: Remote Text Transports Must Negotiate Compression\n   729\t\n   730\tJSON, SSE, JavaScript, CSS, HTML, XML, and SVG served by the portable runner\n   731\tmust honor `Accept-Encoding`. Streaming compression must flush at frame\n   732\tboundaries and preserve backpressure, cancellation, and reconnect behavior.\n   733\t\n   734\t#### Rule 17: Resumable Streams Make Polling A Safety Net\n   735\t\n   736\tWhen SSE provides ids, replay/resync, and cursor wake-ups, polling is not the\n   737\tprimary transport. Poll at a low validation cadence and use fast polling only\n   738\tafter the stream actually fails.\n     1\t# Complete Snapshots Must Not Be Live Frames\n     2\t\n     3\t**Date:** 2026-08-27\n     4\t**Context:** OmniHarness browser-to-runner event transport, especially mobile clients\n     5\t**Symptom:** A phone transferred roughly 6 GB after using OmniHarness for a short period. A live 12-second sample transferred 7,057,338 bytes.\n     6\t**Root Cause:** The SSE route resent an uncompressed 926 KB complete catalog on selected-conversation updates. The catalog held 407 runs, 549 workers, session summaries, plans, and read markers even though only one selected run was changing. Separate five-second snapshot validation and two-to-five-second worker polling added packet churn despite resumable SSE and worker cursors.\n     7\t**Fix:** Keep complete catalog delivery in snapshot bootstrap; project selected-run SSE frames to an explicitly partial catalog; retain the full checksum for cheap `notModified` validation; gzip JSON and SSE with frame flushing; move validation polling to 30–60 second safety intervals.\n     8\t**Verification:** The real runner measured 4,647 wire bytes over the same 12-second window after restart, a 99.93% reduction. `pnpm typecheck`, `pnpm build:interface:web`, focused HTTP/event/client tests, and `pnpm test:lifecycle` verified compilation, streaming flush behavior, scoped merging, cursor recovery, and lifecycle reconnects.\n     9\t**Prevention:** Measure wire bytes, decoded frame sizes, frame cadence, and field-level payload weight before optimizing. ## Task state: task_complete · 2026-08-27T11:05:13.522Z

```text
{
  "type": "task_complete",
  "turn_id": "01a042de-9f1a-73d1-96b6-f93fa8f6eaa8",
  "last_agent_message": "Findings, ordered by severity:\n\n1. [P1] Partial SSE frames can make stale catalog data validate as current — [events.ts](/Users/masterman/NLP/omniharness/src/runtime/http/routes/events.ts:591), [EventStreamStateManager.ts](/Users/masterman/NLP/omniharness/src/interface/home/EventStreamStateManager.ts:159). `buildEventStreamPayload` retains the checksum of the complete server payload, while the client merges only the selected run’s records and stores that checksum. If an unselected run changes, the partial frame advances the client to the new full checksum without delivering that run. The next snapshot poll sends that checksum and receives `notModified`, leaving the unselected run stale indefinitely. The architecture and learning docs currently document this unsafe behavior as intentional at lines 706–707 and line 7. Partial frames must not advance the client’s complete-catalog checksum, or need a distinct scoped checksum.\n\n2. [P2] `Accept-Encoding` negotiation does not correctly honor exclusions — [stream-response.ts](/Users/masterman/NLP/omniharness/src/runtime/http/stream-response.ts:67). It only recognizes the exact string `q=0`, so `gzip;q=0.0` is compressed. It also treats `*` independently, so `gzip;q=0, *;q=1` incorrectly enables gzip despite the explicit exclusion. Additionally, `Vary: Accept-Encoding` is only added when compression is selected at lines 139–143; an identity response for the same compressible resource lacks `Vary`, allowing caches to reuse it across encoding preferences.\n\n3. [P2] The selected-run stream falls back to a complete catalog when the selected run disappears — [events.ts](/Users/masterman/NLP/omniharness/src/runtime/http/routes/events.ts:601). This violates the explicit partial-slice contract and reintroduces the full-catalog transfer on deletion/archive/missing-run paths. The client does need deletion authority, so this needs a scoped tombstone or explicit “complete for this run” contract rather than returning the entire payload.\n\n4. [P2] The focused event-route suite is currently red because its SSE helper loses frames — [events-route.test.ts](/Users/masterman/NLP/omniharness/tests/api/events-route.test.ts:62). Each 200 ms `Promise.race` starts another `reader.read()` without cancelling or retaining the previous read. An abandoned read can consume the update frame, causing the unchanged “streams run and worker state after conversation session sync” test to time out. It failed both in the focused five-file run and alone. This also contradicts the verification claim in [the learning note](/Users/masterman/NLP/omniharness/docs/superpowers/learnings/2026-08-27-complete-snapshots-must-not-be-live-frames.md:8).\n\nVerification:\n\n- `pnpm typecheck`: passed\n- `pnpm build:interface:web`: passed\n- Targeted ESLint: passed\n- Focused tests: 97 passed, 5 skipped, 1 failed as described above\n\nNo files were edited.",
  "started_at": 1787828281,
  "completed_at": 1787828713,
  "duration_ms": 432403,
  "time_to_first_token_ms": 5914
}

## Tool result: call_2b8poEnZGiL06CEqq6c8yBPA · 2026-08-27T11:11:39.217Z

````text
[
  {

  {
    "type": "input_text",
    "text": "---\nname: client-server-state-invariants\ndescription: Use when planning, building, reviewing, or debugging React apps with server state, optimistic UI, caches, polling, SSE/WebSocket streams, background jobs, persistence, or client/server synchronization\n---\n\n# Client Server State Invariants\n\nReact bugs often expose deeper ownership mistakes. When client state, server state, cache state, stream state, and persisted state all exist, every plan needs explicit invariants.\n\n## Core Rule\n\nNo UI surface may treat data as authoritative unless the payload proves ownership, freshness, completeness, ordering, and terminal state.\n\nIf those words are missing from the plan, the plan is probably baking in a race condition.\n\n## Planning Checklist\n\nFor every React app that reads server/runtime state, the plan must answer:\n\n1. **Owner:** What owns this state: server, client manager, URL, cache, stream, local storage, or worker/runtime?\n2. **Token:** What owner token protects async results: `requestId`, `runId`, `projectPath`, `operationId`, `workerId + seq`, or equivalent?\n3. **Provenance:** Is data server-authoritative, optimistic, cached preview, partial update, or historical fallback?\n4. **Completeness:** Does the payload declare what scope it is complete for? What may it erase?\n5. **Ordering:** What stable order is used: monotonic seq, `(createdAt, id)`, version, or cursor?\n6. **State machine:** What are the legal states, terminal states, and transitions?\n7. **Events:** Which server decisions are named events rather than client-side derivations?\n8. **Loading shape:** Which states are bootstrap, first load, incremental refresh, reconnect, resync, recovery, and terminal error?\n9. **Fallbacks:** Which legacy/fallback rows can render, and only after what authority is loaded?\n10. **Tests:** Which race, stale-response, event-sequence, reconnect, and merge tests prove the invariants?\n\n## Invariants To Require\n\n- Cached and optimistic data may pre-render, but cannot satisfy full-load gates.\n- Partial snapshots may add or refine state, but may not erase optimistic objects, delivered user input, read markers, or stream entries unless they declare complete scope.\n- Every async response checks that its owner token still matches before mutating visible state.\n- Mutation success handlers may navigate or clear UI only if they still own the current context, unless the action explicitly creates a new selected context.\n- Timers and polling callbacks re-check ownership immediately before acting.\n- Server decisions are emitted as named events; the client does not infer decisions by diffing snapshots.\n- SSE/WebSocket streams have event ids, replay/resume, and an explicit resync path.\n- Loading booleans are split by lifecycle stage. A leaf stream refresh must not unmount the whole page.\n- Append-only content has one writer, one reader, stable ids, and monotonic cursors.\n- Fallback DB rows or historical rows are gated behind stream loading and deduped by stable id plus safe equivalence.\n- Status classifiers distinguish active, runnable, blocked, awaiting user, terminal, stale, and recoverable states.\n- \"Latest\" queries have deterministic tie-breakers. Timestamp-only ordering is not enough.\n- Hot paths use bounded reads and do not synchronously load large bodies, bridge probes, schema setup, or heavy app graphs.\n- Process-local caches have explicit TTL/size bounds and invalidation rules.\n\n## Test Matrix\n\nPick the rows that match the work. Do not rely on one happy-path UI test.\n\n| Risk | Test shape |\n| --- | --- |\n| Stale async response | Start request A, switch owner, resolve A, assert it cannot mutate B |\n| Optimistic object erased | Create local optimistic state, apply stale/partial snapshot, assert object remains |\n| Cache masquerades as loaded | Hydrate cache preview, assert full-load gate waits for server authority |\n| Mutation steals navigation | Start mutation, switch selection, resolve mutation, assert focus stays put |\n| Timer/poll stale-fire | Use fake timers or deferred promises; callback must re-check owner before acting |\n| Snapshot merge erases stream | Apply partial snapshot missing known entries, assert entries/read markers remain |\n| Event stream gap | Bootstrap snapshot, emit events during disconnect, reconnect with last id, assert replay or resync |\n| Server decision invisible | Exercise branch and assert named event such as `blocked`, `refused`, `gave_up`, or `error.surfaced` |\n| State classifier wrong | Table-test every status into active/runnable/terminal/recoverable buckets |\n| Fallback duplicates stream | Load fallback rows then stream entries, assert no duplicate or false chronology |\n| Hot path bloats | Assert snapshots carry cursors not bodies; route returns before DB/heavy imports when possible |\n| Timestamp tie | Insert equal timestamps, assert deterministic order by id/seq |\n\nUse headless HTTP/SSE tests for control-plane decisions. Use React/unit tests for manager merging and ownership guards. Use Playwright only for real UI layout, focus, scroll, mobile computed styles, and visible affordances.\n\n## Refuse These Shapes\n\n- one `isLoading` boolean for bootstrap, refresh, reconnect, and recovery;\n- cache or optimistic data treated as authoritative without provenance;\n- async callbacks that mutate state without checking owner tokens;\n- client-side derivation of server decisions;\n- snapshot diffs used as the only way to know what happened;\n- event streams without ids or resume;\n- timestamp-only \"latest\" queries;\n- parallel transcript or persistence surfaces that the frontend must reconcile;\n- unbounded process caches introduced to hide slow storage;\n- tests that prove only the DOM result when the bug is a server decision.\n\n## Review Prompt\n\nAsk this before implementation starts:\n\n> What can arrive late, out of order, partially, twice, or after the user changes context, and what test proves it cannot corrupt visible state?\n---\nname: instrumenting-control-planes\ndescription: Use when building, planning, reviewing, or debugging apps with backend decisions, agent workflows, long-running jobs, SSE/WebSocket streams, recovery paths, deletes, retries, or user-visible failures\n---\n\n# Instrumenting Control Planes\n\nBuild the control plane as a product surface. If the server decides, refuses, retries, spawns, deletes, recovers, gives up, or fails, that decision must be observable by users, operators, and tests.\n\nIf a React UI consumes the control plane through server state, optimistic UI, caches, polling, SSE/WebSocket streams, or persisted runtime state, also use `client-server-state-invariants`.\n\n## Core Rule\n\nEvery meaningful server-side state transition emits a named, durable event. The UI and tests consume the same production event surface.\n\nDo not rely on snapshot diffs, rendered DOM state, logs only, or client-side re-derivation to prove what the server decided.\n\n## Hot Path Rule\n\nNo user bootstrap, catalog, status, reconnect, or recovery request may depend on a lock-prone database write path or external health probe finishing. Serve these paths from a bounded read model, memory cache, append-only event log, or stale-but-marked snapshot; queue reconciliation writes outside the request. Every dependency on a database or bridge call in a hot path needs a short timeout, cancellation, and an explicit degraded response.\n\nIf SQLite, local files, or a single-writer store is used, assume writer contention can freeze readers at the worst moment. Use one writer, WAL/busy-timeout discipline, short transactions, bounded queues, and headless chaos tests that run bootstrap/reload while writes are active. A 25s UI request is a control-plane failure, even if the database eventually returns.\n\n## Event Contract\n\nFor each backend or agent workflow, define:\n\n- named events for decisions: `worker.spawned`, `worker.reattached`, `plan.ready`, `plan.review.blocked`, `recovery.gave_up`, `conversation.delete_failed`;\n- payloads with stable ids, `prev`/`next` status when relevant, reason codes, and enough context to debug;\n- `error.surfaced` or an equivalent user-facing failure event for every failure the user should know about;\n- durable persistence or a bounded replay buffer so short disconnects do not erase history;\n- a scriptable read path: CLI, HTTP endpoint, sqlite query, or event log command.\n\nIf a decision cannot be asserted from this surface, the design is incomplete.\n\nIf debugging a control-plane failure reveals a reusable lesson, use `ultrapowers:learning-from-bugs` after the fix is verified.\n\n## Streaming Requirements\n\nFor SSE or WebSocket-like streams:\n\n- emit named events alongside any snapshot/update frame;\n- include monotonic event ids;\n- support resume from the last seen id;\n- replay missed events from a bounded buffer;\n- emit an explicit resync-required event when replay is impossible;\n- bootstrap from a snapshot, then tail events.\n\nSnapshots show current truth. Named events show why it became true.\n\n## Testing Standard\n\nControl-plane tests should usually be headless HTTP/SSE scenario tests, not browser tests. Browser automation is for UI behavior; control-plane tests assert server decisions directly.\n\nWrite scenario assertions as event transcripts:\n\n```ts\nexpect(events).toHaveEmitted(\"worker.spawned\", { runId });\nexpect(events).toHaveEmitted(\"worker.status\", { next: \"running\" });\nchaos.killServer();\nchaos.restartServer();\nexpect(events).toHaveEmitted(\"worker.reattached\", { runId });\nexpect(events).not.toHaveEmitted(\"worker.recreated\", { runId });\n```\n\nChaos belongs outside production code paths: drop connections in the client, fail fetches in the harness, restart the server process, and use seeds for reproducibility.\n\n## Refuse These Shapes\n\n- silent early returns in server methods;\n- swallowed `catch` blocks;\n- client-side derivation of server decisions;\n- response-code-only user failures;\n- event streams without ids or resume;\n- tests that infer control-plane behavior from rendered state;\n- delete handlers that do not enumerate dependent rows or emit typed failure events;\n- recovery loops without terminal events such as `gave_up` or `abandoned`;\n- request handlers that wait on inline reconciliation writes, SQLite contention, or bridge `/doctor` checks before returning bootstrap data;\n- test-only behavior branches in production code.\n\n## Design Checklist\n\nBefore implementing a backend or agent workflow, answer:\n\n1. What decisions can this workflow make?\n2. What named event is emitted for each decision, including refusal and failure?\n3. Where are events persisted or buffered?\n4. How does a client resume after disconnect?\n5. Which request paths must keep working while persistence or bridge dependencies are slow, locked, or stale?\n6. How can a CLI or test harness inspect the exact event sequence without the UI?\n7. Which headless scenario proves the happy path, failure path, recovery path, terminal state, and hot-path timeout/degraded path?\n8. Which client state consumes these events, and how does it prove ownership, freshness, completeness, ordering, and replay/resync?\n\nIf the answer is \"the UI can figure it out\", name the missing event instead.\n"
  }
]

One pre-existing event-route session-sync test remains red in isolation and is tracked separately from this transport change.\n     9\t**Prevention:** Measure wire bytes, decoded frame sizes, frame cadence, and field-level payload weight before optimizing. Require every run-scoped stream to declare catalog completeness, require compression at the portable HTTP boundary, and treat polling as recovery rather than the normal transport when resumable events exist.\n    10\t**Skill/Doc Updates:** Updated `docs/architecture/hot-path-responsiveness-and-resource-leaks.md` with the incident evidence and three permanent transport rules. No shared skill needed changes because the existing control-plane and client/server skills already require scoped completeness, cursors instead of bodies, resumable SSE, and bounded hot paths; the missing rule was project-specific enforcement at the HTTP boundary.\n"
